### Placement notes

I reviewed placement only. No files were changed. The reconciled map has 30 packages to promote now, plus 4 deferred until a consumer exists (npm platform half, sigstore, ink, and engine folded into mcp-kit). That covers all 28 promotable modules. Five modules split: npm, sbom, tsconfig-json, cli and github-actions.

**Where I disagree with the provisional D12 homes:**
- These move from tooling/library to foundation/capability because drivers or capability packages need to import them: walker, env (as @beep/runtime-env), config-file, xdg and cli (as @beep/cli-kit).
- templates moves from tooling/library to foundation/modeling as @beep/managed-section.
- npm and sbom (both provisionally drivers) split. The pure halves go to modeling. Only the SDK, binary or service code stays a driver, and that code is deferred until something consumes it.
- tsconfig-json splits. The schemas go to modeling. The loader goes to tooling/library because a modeling package cannot import walker, and every consumer of the loader is tooling.
- These provisional homes hold as proposed: commands, git, github and github-actions as drivers; workspaces as tooling/library (as @beep/repo-workspaces); memfs as tooling/test-kit.

**Ceiling check:** I checked every kit-import edge against these placements and each one is legal.
- The workspaces edges to the commands and git drivers rely on the tooling operational-adapter clause, so the manifest must declare both drivers directly.
- I found one problem the proposals missed. github-actions' PackageManagerInstaller imports DEFAULT_REGISTRY, which is defined in npm/NpmRegistry.ts (the driver half). The constant has to move into @beep/npm-spec. Otherwise github-actions needs the npm driver.
- IdentityToken stays in @beep/sbom, so github-actions has no edge to Sigstore.

**Two gates the proposals understated:**
- **Capability gate (07 "Specific Homes Before Capability"):** a capability package needs at least two importers that already import it, named in its README. walker, runtime-env, config-file, xdg and cli-kit all have zero repo importers today. Each promotion PR therefore has to migrate at least two named consumers at the same time, or land alongside the kit packages that depend on it.
- **Upstream-first rule (ARCHITECTURE.md:779-785, DECISIONS 2026-09-29):** yaml, toml and jsonl each need a logged facet census against effect/encoding/{Yaml, Toml, Ndjson} before the package is created.

**Workspace cycles:** @beep/jsonc, @beep/glob and @beep/semver import @beep/schema. The matching @beep/schema concepts (Jsonc, Glob, Semver) and the @beep/utils/Glob matching therefore have to be deleted, not re-implemented on top of the new packages. Delete them in the PRs that migrate their consumers, with no alias left behind.

**Ecosystem:** none of these packages qualifies as-is. Ecosystem members may not carry any @beep/* runtime dependency, and every kit module depends on identity and schema.

**Markdown (D1):** I gave it a modeling home that works either way. I recommend merging it into @beep/md so there is only one Markdown AST owner. A sibling @beep/markdown is also ceiling-legal.

Key doctrine anchors are in standards/ARCHITECTURE.md:
- 470-487: routing order and the capability gate
- 592-638: family ceilings and the tooling driver-adapter clause
- 779-785: upstream-first rule

The routing order also appears in standards/architecture/07-non-slice-families.md:32-90.

### Promotion waves

**Wave 1**: @beep/jsonc, @beep/toml, @beep/yaml, @beep/jsonl, @beep/semver, @beep/spdx, @beep/schema-org, @beep/glob, @beep/github-references, @beep/github-commands, @beep/managed-section, @beep/memfs, @beep/commands, @beep/git

No kit dependencies. memfs comes first because later suites devDepend on it. The yaml, toml and jsonl facet censuses must be logged before these packages are created. D2 must be ruled before @beep/commands is created. In the same PRs: retire @beep/schema/Jsonc and @beep/schema/Semver and migrate their consumers.

**Wave 2**: @beep/npm-spec, @beep/walker, @beep/markdown (or @beep/md merge), @beep/tsconfig, @beep/github, @beep/runtime-env

@beep/github lands with the repo-cli migration and drops @effected/github. walker lands with repo-utils Root/FsUtils as named importers. runtime-env lands with repo-cli EnvConfig and/or chalk as importers (cli-kit arrives in wave 4). Retire @beep/schema/Glob and @beep/utils/Glob matching here.

**Wave 3**: @beep/package-json, @beep/lockfiles, @beep/config-file, @beep/github-reporting, @beep/repo-tsconfig

repo-tsconfig replaces get-tsconfig. config-file names docgen and the repo-cli Models store as importers.

**Wave 4**: @beep/sbom, @beep/xdg, @beep/cli-kit, @beep/repo-workspaces

cli-kit needs runtime-env, walker and github-commands. workspaces needs commands, git, lockfiles and package-json.

**Wave 5**: @beep/github-actions

A leaf driver. Needs github, sbom, walker and npm-spec.

**Wave 6**: @beep/npm (deferred), @beep/sigstore (deferred), @beep/ink (deferred), @beep/mcp-kit engine fold (deferred)

Each lands with its first real consumer (mcpb-kit tarball, a release-attestation step, Accounts.tui, an MCP server ProcessGuard). Under S31 no package is created ahead of its consumer.

### Retire

- scratchpad/effected/runner (the port's audit runner; it imports @beep/repo-cli and is not a promotion candidate), scratchpad/effected/audit.ts, scratchpad/effected/tsconfig.lab.json and the per-module scratchpad/effected/<m> + scratchpad/test/<m> trees, each deleted in the PR that promotes it; PORT_LEDGER.json, OPPORTUNITIES.md, TESTS_NOT_PASSING.md, DIAGNOSTIC_EXCEPTIONS.md and CACHE_BASELINE_REVIEW.md archived into the program goal packet
- $ScratchpadId uses in kit sources, replaced by per-package ids registered by create-package; the $ScratchpadId composer in packages/foundation/modeling/identity/src/packages.ts and its shape-stable.test.ts assertion only once no scratchpad importer remains; the matching standards/jsdoc-documentation.inventory.* rows
- @effected/github: root package.json:41 catalog entry and packages/tooling/tool/cli/package.json:232, retired once cli src/internal/github/* and commands/Yeet/internal/GhOps.ts import @beep/github (wave 2); transitive @effected/* entries leave bun.lock
- @beep/schema/Jsonc, @beep/schema/Semver, @beep/schema/Glob concepts, and @beep/utils/Glob matching, retired in their consumer-migration PRs with no alias
- repo-cli internal/cli/Jsonc.ts, internal/GlobPattern.ts, and @beep/repo-configs AllowlistJsoncTextToUnknown (reuse phase)
- @beep/test-utils/MemoryFileSystem (if D5 is ruled as recommended)
- npm deps after reuse migration: jsonc-parser, get-tsconfig, the 'toml' runtime/shim use, 'yaml' runtime deps in repo-cli and ai-metrics (it stays as an oracle devDep of @beep/yaml), picomatch/tinyglobby (after walker covers multi-pattern expansion); root spdx-* devDeps move to @beep/spdx devDeps
- goals/effect-child-process-hardening/SPEC.md:101 'Driver-local output folds' exception row, retired explicitly in its Decision Log if D2 is ruled as recommended

## Operator decisions

### D1: Markdown: merge the kit markdown module into @beep/md, or ship it as a sibling @beep/markdown?

Options:
- Merge into @beep/md as new concept modules (parser, frontmatter codecs, edits)
- Sibling @beep/markdown in foundation/modeling, with a later bridge to the @beep/md AST
- Sibling now, merge later

Recommendation: Merge into @beep/md.

Reason: Two Markdown AST owners in foundation/modeling is a second-owner smell under the upstream-first and no-alias rulings. Both placements are ceiling-legal: jsonc, toml and yaml never import @beep/md. github-reporting depends on whichever package wins.

### D2: Where does the commands process runner live, and does it supersede the effect-child-process-hardening ruling that 'forbids a new shared process facade' (goals/effect-child-process-hardening/SPEC.md:101)?

Options:
- drivers/commands (wraps external binaries generically)
- foundation/capability/commands
- Do not promote; keep driver-local folds

Recommendation: drivers/commands. Retire the SPEC exception row explicitly in that packet's Decision Log, citing its reconsider condition.

Reason: Routing step 2 comes before capability. Drivers (git, npm, ffmpeg and others) and tooling (via the adapter clause) can all reach it. Nothing in foundation or the slices spawns processes.

### D3: cli-kit home: foundation/capability @beep/cli-kit, or tooling/library @beep/repo-cli-kit?

Options:
- foundation/capability @beep/cli-kit
- tooling/library @beep/repo-cli-kit

Recommendation: foundation/capability.

Reason: Product apps (practice-setup, practice-gateway, docket-intake and others) and drivers would consume it. A tooling home turns tooling into a backdoor dependency root, which ARCHITECTURE.md forbids. It has no external framework dependency once ./ui is split out. Record the reading that terminal presentation is not a ui-system role.

### D4: Does jsonl's Journal (a stateful watched-append service) stay in @beep/jsonl (modeling)?

Options:
- Keep it as a ./Journal subpath of @beep/jsonl
- Split it to a capability package once it has two adopters
- Defer Journal and promote only the pure codecs

Recommendation: Keep it as a ./Journal subpath, never re-exported from the root.

Reason: Its deps are effect and modeling only, so the ceiling holds. Abstract-FileSystem services have precedent in modeling (utils/FileSystem.ts, ontology/TaxonomyLoader.ts). A capability split would fail the at-least-two-importers gate today.

### D5: In-memory FileSystem ownership: a dedicated @beep/memfs, or replace the engine inside @beep/test-utils/MemoryFileSystem?

Options:
- Dedicated @beep/memfs (test-kit) and retire @beep/test-utils/MemoryFileSystem
- Replace the engine in place inside @beep/test-utils
- Keep both

Recommendation: Dedicated @beep/memfs, then retire the test-utils copy in the PR that migrates its 64 consumers. 'Keep both' is not acceptable.

Reason: Both are the same upstream engine (PR #6573), so only one owner may remain. A dedicated package keeps consumers from pulling in test-utils' pg/testcontainers deps. Resolve the copy-overwrite error-path divergence against FileSystemConformance first. Naming follows the @beep/test-utils and @beep/fc-runs test-kit precedent, not @beep/repo-*.

### D6: Promote the platform halves that have no consumer (@beep/npm driver, @beep/sigstore driver, @beep/ink driver) now, or defer them?

Options:
- Promote everything now so scratchpad empties
- Defer each until its first consumer and leave those files in scratchpad
- Defer and archive the files to the program packet

Recommendation: Defer each until its first consumer. Leave the files in scratchpad, marked as pending.

Reason: S31 (no inventory growth) and the routing rules favour consumer-backed packages. None of these sits on another package's dependency path after the npm-spec and sbom splits.

### D7: Which package.json model wins: @beep/package-json or @beep/repo-utils schemas/PackageJson.ts (2,125 LOC)?

Options:
- @beep/package-json owns the generic fields; repo-utils keeps only the beep family/kind metadata via Package.extend
- Keep repo-utils as the strict model and use the kit only for edits
- Keep both

Recommendation: @beep/package-json owns the generic fields. repo-utils keeps the beep metadata as an extension and the JSON Schema emission.

Reason: Generic field models are duplicated today. Under the 'drivers never import tooling' rule, the reusable model must live in foundation.

### D8: Which tsconfig model wins: @beep/tsconfig (passthrough, tsc merge semantics) or @beep/repo-utils TSConfig.ts (strict, closed)?

Options:
- @beep/tsconfig wins; the strict checks become a validator in @beep/repo-tsconfig
- Keep both (strict for docgen, kit for resolution)

Recommendation: @beep/tsconfig wins, and the strict checks move to a validator.

Reason: Two document models give one capability two owners. docgen is the only consumer of the strict model.

### D9: github-actions reporting suite: split into @beep/github-reporting (modeling), or keep it as a ./reporting subpath of the driver?

Options:
- Split into foundation/modeling
- Keep as a driver subpath

Recommendation: Split.

Reason: These files are pure text models. The routing table sends pure grammars to modeling. The driver root is not browser-safe, and the repo-cli Reviewer and CI summaries are named reuse sites.

### D10: Publish any of these as ecosystem packages?

Options:
- None now
- Publish selected format engines (jsonc, toml, yaml, glob) under packages/ecosystem/*

Recommendation: None now.

Reason: Ecosystem members may carry no @beep/* runtime dep, and every module depends on @beep/identity, @beep/schema and @beep/utils. Publishing would need a de-beeped rewrite through the 14-ecosystem-packages gate. Reopen only with an explicit publication goal.

### D11: engine: fold into @beep/mcp-kit, ship a standalone @beep/tool-engine, or defer?

Options:
- Defer, then fold into @beep/mcp-kit on first adoption
- Fold into mcp-kit now
- Standalone foundation/capability package

Recommendation: Defer, then fold into mcp-kit when the first MCP server adopts ProcessGuard or LaunchContext.

Reason: It has zero importers today. A standalone package fails the capability gate. mcp-kit already carries the same deps.

### D12: Upstream-first gate for yaml, toml and jsonl against effect/encoding/{Yaml,Toml,Ndjson}.

Options:
- Log a facet census per package (the uncovered facets dominate), then create the package
- Skip the census

Recommendation: Log the census in the program packet's decision log before the wave-1 PRs.

Reason: ARCHITECTURE.md:779-785 and DECISIONS 2026-09-29 forbid a new modeling concept where upstream covers its intent. Upstream parse is a throwing subset, so the census will justify the packages, but it must be on record.

### D13: Package names that need confirmation: @beep/runtime-env (vs @beep/env), @beep/managed-section (vs @beep/templates), @beep/npm-spec, @beep/commands (vs @beep/process-runner).

Options:
- Accept the proposed names
- Keep the upstream names for diffability

Recommendation: Accept the proposed names. Keep @beep/commands.

Reason: The upstream 'templates' and 'env' names mislead (templates is not a template engine; env reads like config loading). 'commands' collides with no package name, only with the repo-cli src/commands directory.

### D14: Colour-detection owner and precedence once @beep/runtime-env exists (@beep/chalk SupportsColor and @beep/colors disagree with Node's FORCE_COLOR-first order).

Options:
- runtime-env owns the colour-level decision; chalk and colors delegate
- Keep three detectors

Recommendation: runtime-env owns it, with FORCE_COLOR-first Node precedence.

Reason: One owner per capability. This is decided in the reuse phase, not at promotion.

### Markdown open questions

#### MQ1: What should the syntax-layer package be named?

Options:
- @beep/markdown
- @beep/md-syntax
- @beep/mdast

Recommendation: @beep/markdown. It matches upstream, keeps the import rewrite mechanical, and follows the foundation @beep/<purpose> rule. @beep/mdast is inaccurate because the nodes deliberately depart from mdast and the package also parses, formats and edits.

#### MQ2: Where should typed frontmatter and the YAML/TOML/JSON codecs live?

Options:
- Subpaths of @beep/markdown, landed after yaml/toml/jsonc are promoted
- A separate @beep/frontmatter package
- Subpaths of @beep/md

Recommendation: Subpaths of @beep/markdown, in a follow-up PR. Frontmatter.ts already imports MarkdownDocument and MarkdownEdit, so a separate package buys nothing. Putting them in @beep/md would push the yaml, toml and jsonc deps into every slice.

#### MQ3: Should Md.Document frontmatter ever render as YAML or TOML instead of ---json?

Options:
- No: keep ---json for persistence and use the @beep/markdown source layer for typed and edited frontmatter
- Yes: add an additive Document.frontmatterFormat field with an injected codec in renderWith

Recommendation: No for now. Revisit only if a generator needs YAML output from a Document. If so, use the additive field with an injected codec so that @beep/md never imports yaml.

#### MQ4: What should the bridge do with parsed HTML by default?

Options:
- escapeAsText
- rawHtml (SafeDocument then rejects it)
- drop

Recommendation: escapeAsText. Raw HTML can still be requested explicitly through the trusted path.

#### MQ5: Where should GitHub > [!KIND] admonitions and $$ math be recognized?

Options:
- A 'beep' dialect registry entry in @beep/markdown (parser and stringifier)
- Post-parse recognizers in the @beep/md bridge
- Not supported

Recommendation: Bridge recognizers in phase 1, behind options that default to on. Then a beep dialect registry entry once the bridge-corpus ledger exists, so that round-trips become exact.

#### MQ6: Should Md.render eventually become the canonical stringifier (stringify plus a URL-policy transform), accepting a one-time output churn in docgen, Yeet and skill output?

Options:
- Yes, after a differential test bounds the drift
- Keep both writers permanently

Recommendation: Decide later, based on evidence. Land the differential test between the two writers now. Converge only if the drift is small and the URL policy transform is proven equivalent.

#### MQ7: Should @beep/schema's micromark-backed Markdown and MarkdownTextToHtml brands move onto the new parser?

Options:
- Move them into @beep/markdown as a MarkdownText brand and deprecate them in @beep/schema
- Keep them on micromark as a Bun-parity validator

Recommendation: Keep micromark for now. @beep/markdown depends on @beep/schema, so rehoming must go one way (into @beep/markdown) to avoid a cycle, and HTML parity is not proven.

#### MQ8: What should happen to the MDX node classes, which are build/serialize-only and have no repo consumer?

Options:
- Keep them behind a separate subpath
- Drop them at promotion

Recommendation: Keep them behind a separate subpath (for example ./MarkdownMdx), out of the default surface. They count in the 115-export ledger, and dropping them later is cheap.

#### MQ9: Should the promotion re-verify the README-vs-ledger contradictions (req-2 and req-8 heading-hash loss) and schedule req-9 and req-12 (Data.TaggedError carriers) before or after PR 1?

Options:
- Before PR 1 (block promotion)
- In the same PR as promotion
- As tracked follow-ups after promotion

Recommendation: Re-verify req-2 and req-8 before PR 1, because a heading bug in a stable package costs more. Migrate the carriers in the same PR, because they are small and are a repo-law violation.

## Markdown merge

### Judge recommendation

Adopt the Layered design, with corrections. Overall scores: Layered 8.4, Facade 6.6, Canonical 5.0.

Per-criterion scores (strengths / capability / coherence / disruption / architecture / tests / incremental):
- Layered: 9 / 9 / 7 / 10 / 9 / 9 / 9
- Facade: 9 / 9 / 5 / 6 / 7 / 8 / 6
- Canonical: 5 / 10 / 10 / 2 / 7 / 8 / 3

Shape:
1. Create @beep/markdown at packages/foundation/modeling/markdown with `bun run beep create-package`. Promote the port's 13 public modules nearly verbatim. Re-key the 25 $ScratchpadId sites to a new package identity and rewrite the about 300 @beep/scratchpad doc imports. Move the tests, the 548K of fixtures, the 5 canon exceptions and the commonmark@0.31.2 devDependency.
2. Do not create @beep/frontmatter. Add the typed-frontmatter modules (Frontmatter, FrontmatterResolver, and the Yaml/Toml/JsonFrontmatter codecs) to @beep/markdown as subpaths in a follow-up PR once @beep/yaml, @beep/toml and @beep/jsonc exist. FrontmatterSource ships in the first PR. The layered design's stated reason for a separate package does not hold: scratchpad/effected/markdown/Frontmatter.ts:20-21 imports MarkdownDocument and MarkdownEdit, so typed frontmatter is not parser-free. Subpaths give the same promotion-order decoupling with one fewer package.
3. In @beep/md, add only the subpaths Md.syntax (fromMarkdownDocument bridge plus loss and source-map schemas) and Md.parse (parse / parseSafe). Keep them out of src/index.ts, which re-exports every module via `export *`, and out of the Md object at Md.ts:1333-1394, which apps import. Enforce this with an import-graph test.
4. Persisted Md.model stays wire-identical. Document is used at workspace/domain Message.model.ts:9, ThreadStore.repo.internal.ts:10 and Chat.rpc.ts:13.

Verified against source:
- Md.render.ts:113-117 emits frontmatter only as ---json.
- Md.model.ts:3486 Document is {_tag, frontmatter: OptionFromOptionalKey(JsonObject), children}.
- MarkdownNode uses S.Class (Point :59, Position :84, Heading :1032, Root :1817).
- internal/carriers.ts:117,205 still use Data.TaggedError.
- Only the three codec files import sibling modules (../yaml, ../toml, ../jsonc).
- md runtime deps are html, identity, schema and utils; micromark is a devDependency only.

The canonical single-AST design stays as a later, evidence-gated convergence option, not the starting point. The bridge-corpus ledger and the round-trip properties produce the evidence that would justify or reject it.

### Option scores

| option | score |
|---|---|
| Facade-first: one @beep/md package, two node layers (Md.source + Md.model) joined by lower/lift | 6.6 |
| Layered: new @beep/markdown syntax package + @beep/md bridge (+ optional @beep/frontmatter) | 8.4 |
| Canonical: one mdast-shaped AST (effected MarkdownNode) replaces Md.model | 5 |

#### Facade-first: one @beep/md package, two node layers (Md.source + Md.model) joined by lower/lift (6.6)

Folds the whole effected port into @beep/md as new subpaths (Md.source, Md.parse, Md.document, Md.edit, Md.frontmatter[.yaml|.toml|.json], Md.visit, Md.lower, Md.lift, Md.mdast). Md.model stays the persisted wire; lower = Source.Root -> Document with sourceMap and losses; lift = Document -> Source.Root with synthetic positions. The Md namespace object gains parse, lower, lift, edit and frontmatter.

Pros:
- One import surface and one identity root ($MdId)
- Additive Document.frontmatterFormat keeps today's ---json output byte-identical while allowing YAML or TOML later
- documentSafetyIssuesWithSource reports safety violations with line and column through the shared path vocabulary
- Proposes semantic fold/transform over Md.model to replace the while/Reflect.get walk in Md.safe, and one shared section algorithm
- Frozen Document.Encoded wire corpus test as a regression gate

Cons:
- Two 'Heading' and 'Table' class families in one package, which this repo's naming laws cannot easily police
- The jsonc, toml and yaml codec deps join the dependency closure of agents/domain, workspace/domain and law-practice/domain
- Putting parse in the Md namespace object is a real bundle leak, because index.ts re-exports everything and the Md object is the common import
- md package gates (package-verify, docgen, coverage baseline) get much slower for every md consumer
- P2 (the codecs) is serialized behind the jsonc, toml and yaml promotions inside the most heavily consumed modeling package

#### Layered: new @beep/markdown syntax package + @beep/md bridge (+ optional @beep/frontmatter) (8.4)

Promote the port almost verbatim as @beep/markdown (foundation/modeling) for parsing, positions, MarkdownDocument queries, MarkdownEdit, MarkdownFormat, the canonical stringifier, mdast interop, the visitor and FrontmatterSource. @beep/md stays the authoring, persistence, trust and render model. It gains Md.syntax (fromMarkdownDocument -> MdFromSyntax{document, losses, sourceMap}) and Md.parse (parse -> bridge -> refineSafeDocument) as subpath-only modules that are kept out of index.ts and the Md object. Typed frontmatter and its codecs go into a separate @beep/frontmatter package.

Pros:
- Zero wire migration and zero disruption for the persistence, RPC and editor paths
- Core @beep/markdown can be promoted immediately: only YamlFrontmatter.ts:1, TomlFrontmatter.ts:1 and JsonFrontmatter.ts:1 import siblings
- Explicit bundle-leak guard (a hard rule plus a test) that keeps the parser out of @beep/md's index.ts and Md object
- Best test story: a bridge-corpus ledger (data/conformance/bridge-corpus.json) and the property fromMarkdownDocument(parse(Md.render(d))) ≈ normalize(d), which finally tests Md.render against real parsing
- MarkdownRegion collapses the marker-splice family (Yeet Provenance/GhOps, Models, Quality, Findings.refresh, Atlas) into one helper
- Keeps parser conformance (@beep/markdown) separate from semantic validity (Md.conformance) in the docs, which is honest

Cons:
- The @beep/frontmatter split rationale is weak: scratchpad Frontmatter.ts:20-21 imports MarkdownDocument and MarkdownEdit, so typed frontmatter needs the parser anyway
- Two Markdown serializers (Md.render and stringify) and two visitor APIs remain until a later phase
- Declines to share the section fold with rdf/MdSections and leaves Md's traversal gap (gap 7) for a later PR
- md vs markdown naming confusion; duplicate class names across the two trees
- Double allocation when bridging, so bulk tooling must stay on the syntax layer

#### Canonical: one mdast-shaped AST (effected MarkdownNode) replaces Md.model (5)

The effected MarkdownNode tree, converted to S.Opaque, extended with admonition, embed, math and inlineMath, and given optional wire positions, becomes the only Markdown AST. @beep/markdown holds syntax and the AST. @beep/md becomes an authoring and trust layer whose builders return canonical nodes. Md.model, RawMarkdown, HeadingValue, TaskList and YouTube are deleted after a jsonb data migration with a down-migration and a major changeset.

Pros:
- One tree for parse, build, render, safe, edit and interop; no duplicate section algorithms or serializers in the end state
- Spread, definitions and reference links become representable, so HTML output gains spec fidelity
- Its safe-profile corpus property is excellent: every CommonMark and GFM example under BrowserSafe yields SafeHtml or a typed violation, never a throw
- remark/mdast interop comes for free

Cons:
- Breaks the repo's `_tag` discriminator convention and forces a jsonb migration of live chat history
- Rewrites about 30 instanceof sites in the proven engine and risks parse performance (schema construction already dominates parse cost)
- Removing RawMarkdown changes verbatim passthrough semantics for docgen and JSDoc content
- Long coexistence window (steps 4-9) recreates the two-AST state it set out to avoid
- Core @beep/markdown would depend on jsonc, toml and yaml directly, gating the core promotion
- Cannot land incrementally without dual-read shims

### Capability table

| capability | @beep/md | effected markdown | merged home |
|---|---|---|---|
| Parse Markdown text to an AST | None. No string-to-Document path exists (README.md:23-25); ingest only via the pandoc binary + Pandoc.mapping.ts:10 | Markdown.parse/parseResult; CommonMark 0.31.2 + GFM; 652/652 examples, empty skip map | @beep/markdown/Markdown (syntax tree); @beep/md/Md.parse for parse -> Md.Document |
| Source positions and offsets | None (semantic AST) | Point/Position on every node, UTF-16 offsets | @beep/markdown/MarkdownNode; Md gets them only via the MdSourceMap side table in Md.syntax |
| Surgical offset edits and managed regions | None | MarkdownEdit.applyAll, MarkdownFormat.modify | @beep/markdown/MarkdownEdit + new MarkdownRegion helper; replacement content rendered by Md.render |
| Canonical formatting / normalization | Md.render emits its own Markdown style | MarkdownFormat.format, canonical stringify (a published contract) | @beep/markdown/MarkdownFormat; the single-writer question deferred to a later phase |
| Document queries (headings, sections, links, find) | None. rdf/Adapters/MdSections.ts hand-folds sections | MarkdownDocument.headings/sections/sectionByHeading/links/find/findAll | @beep/markdown/MarkdownDocument; section algorithm shared with rdf/MdSections via internal sectionsBy |
| Frontmatter | Option<JsonObject>, rendered only as ---json (Md.render.ts:113-117) | Byte-exact FrontmatterSource split/join; typed schema(S, codec); set/setToString; YAML/TOML/JSON codecs; $schema resolver | FrontmatterSource in the @beep/markdown core (PR 1); Frontmatter/Resolver/codecs as @beep/markdown subpaths after yaml/toml/jsonc land; Md.Document.frontmatter unchanged for persistence |
| Builder DSL (tagged templates, Md.make, Md.table) | Full DSL (Md.ts:544-1394) | Node make() only | @beep/md/Md (unchanged) |
| Render to Markdown, HTML string or plain text | Result-returning render/renderHtml/renderPlainText with adapters and URL policy | stringify only; HTML out of scope | @beep/md/Md.render (unchanged); stringify stays the syntax-tree writer |
| Trust boundary / safe HTML | SafeDocument brand, typed violations, UrlPolicySpec, renderSafeHtml via @beep/html | None (MAX_NESTING_DEPTH hardening only) | @beep/md/Md.safe + Md.html; parsed text enters via Md.parse.parseSafe; source-located violations via documentSafetyIssuesWithSource |
| Spec conformance | Semantic invariants only (4/8/6 checked); no corpus run | Parser corpus, GFM sections, dialect matrix, mdast fixtures, oracle, pathological suite | Parser conformance in @beep/markdown; semantic profiles in @beep/md plus a new bridge-corpus ledger (data/conformance/bridge-corpus.json) and the safe-profile corpus property |
| Traversal / visitor | None. Each consumer writes its own walk | MarkdownVisitor.visit (a Stream of Enter/Exit events) | @beep/markdown/MarkdownVisitor for the syntax tree; new @beep/md Md.visit fold/transform for the semantic tree |
| mdast / remark interop | None | toMdast / fromMdast (a checked boundary) | @beep/markdown/Mdast |
| Pattern Ontology po annotations | On every node; feeds the pandoc and lexical proofs | None | Stays on Md.model; @beep/markdown is documented as outside the PO proof |
| Persistence / RPC wire | Document (jsonb in workspace, SafeDocument in Chat.rpc) | Not a wire format | @beep/md/Md.model (wire-identical; additive changes only) |

### Grafted ideas

- From Facade: semantic fold/transform/nodes combinators over Md.model, keyed by DocumentSafetyPathSegment. Then rewrite Md.safe boundedDocumentNodeCount (the while + Reflect.get walk) and the conformance inspector on top of them. This closes gap 7 in its own PR.
- From Facade: documentSafetyIssuesWithSource(lowered), which joins Md.safe violations to the bridge sourceMap to report line and column. This is the KnowledgeDoctor-style diagnostic win.
- From Facade: a frozen Document.Encoded wire corpus, captured from today's version, that must decode and re-encode byte-identically after every bridge PR.
- From Facade: an additive Document.frontmatterFormat Option, with absent meaning the legacy ---json. Defer it until an operator decides that persisted docs need YAML output, and keep the yaml codec injected rather than imported by Md.render.
- From Facade: one shared heading-section algorithm (internal sectionsBy) used by both rdf/Adapters/MdSections.ts and MarkdownDocument.sections. The layered design rejected this, but rdf is modeling, and a generic fold parameterized by a heading accessor creates no coupling to the syntax tree.
- From Canonical: the safe-profile corpus property. For all 652 CommonMark examples plus GFM, run parse -> bridge -> refineSafeDocument -> renderSafeHtml under BrowserSafe. Each must yield SafeHtml or a typed DocumentSafetyViolation, never a throw.
- From Canonical: migrate DocumentSection to a schema (ledger back-1) and migrate the MarkdownVisitor Data.taggedEnum events to a schema tagged union at promotion. Schedule req-9 and req-12 (the carriers) in the same wave.
- From Canonical and Facade: fix Md.html.ts:43 (Effect.runSync inside renderSafeHtml) by exposing a Result twin, renderSafeHtmlResult, and keeping the throwing mirror.
- From Canonical: a 'beep' dialect registry entry in @beep/markdown for GitHub > [!KIND] alerts and $$ math (registry-only, as planned for obsidian), as the principled long-term home. Bridge-level recognizers are the phase-1 fallback.
- From Facade: P5 convergence decision on a single Markdown writer (Md.render as policyTransform + stringify ∘ lift). Gate it on the differential test between the two serializers; never assert it.

## Module readiness notes

### jsonc

- **readiness**: ["Tests: 11 test files in scratchpad/test/jsonc (252+ tests per ledger S3, 100% per-file coverage, 40-property floor); no fixtures dir (fixturesBytes 0).", "PORT_LEDGER row w0-jsonc: done, 12 deviations (pinned by tests, e.g. typed SerializationFailed instead of thrown RangeError, multi-line insert indent fix), 13 backlog rows (all style/editorial, e.g. fable-2-4: six two-data-arg facade statics lack a pipeable form).", "Exports: 29 public names (28 expected + JsoncEditOverlapError); proposed subpaths ./Jsonc ./JsoncEdit ./JsoncFingerprint ./JsoncFormatter ./JsoncModifier ./JsoncNode ./JsoncVisitor, \"./internal/*\": null.", "Manifest deps to add: effect, @beep/identity, @beep/schema, @beep/utils (runtime); devDeps @effect/vitest, @beep/fc-runs, @beep/test-runner (tests use node:crypto; NodeCrypto layer appears only in docs/tests).", "Identity: replace $ScratchpadId.create(\"effected/jsonc/...\") with the $JsoncId composer that create-package registers in @beep/identity/packages; error/class identity strings change.", "Imports: JSDoc examples import @beep/scratchpad/effected/jsonc/index (62 occurrences) -> @beep/jsonc/<Concept>; tests import ../../effected/jsonc/index.ts and internal/{navigate,parser,scanner,skip} -> @beep/jsonc and @beep/jsonc/internal/* test aliases (packages/**/test law forbids relative imports into src).", "LICENSE (MIT, C. Spencer Beggs) present in module dir; keep README Attribution (Microsoft jsonc-parser design notices in internal/scanner.ts and internal/parser.ts headers).", "Do not carry scratchpad/effected/jsonc/.review/ (review briefs) or tsconfig.json; KNOWLEDGE.md is upstream verbatim and references okf/ paths of the reference checkout.", "Repo-law friction to expect: README says pure statics; JsoncFingerprint needs Crypto in R (fine); docgen example typecheck must be re-run on new import paths."]
- **purpose**: JSONC parse (values or offset-preserving JsoncNode AST), strip comments, byte-minimal edits (JsoncEdit), formatter, modify-by-path, Stream visitor, Schema codecs (Jsonc.fromString / Jsonc.schema / JsoncFromString), canonical fingerprint/hash. Zero third-party deps; scanner/parser vendored with attribution to Microsoft jsonc-parser design (MIT).
- **proposedPackages**: [{"name": "@beep/jsonc", "dir": "packages/foundation/modeling/jsonc", "family": "foundation", "kind": "modeling", "contents": "All of scratchpad/effected/jsonc: Jsonc.ts, JsoncEdit.ts, JsoncFingerprint.ts, JsoncFormatter.ts, JsoncModifier.ts, JsoncNode.ts, JsoncVisitor.ts as flat concept subpaths (./Jsonc, ./JsoncEdit, ...), internal/* behind \"./internal/*\": null like @beep/md; LICENSE (MIT, C. Spencer Beggs) + README Attribution section kept. Tests from scratchpad/test/jsonc -> packages/foundation/modeling/jsonc/test.", "rationale": "A pure format grammar with runtime deps only on foundation/modeling packages (@beep/identity, @beep/schema/LiteralKit, @beep/utils/thunk) satisfies the modeling ceiling (primitive+modeling). Root must be runtime-neutral/browser-safe (ARCHITECTURE 'Driver package roots are not browser-safe by default. Foundation package roots must be runtime-neutral') and it is. Domain slices may only import primitive/modeling, and JSONC config decoding is useful to slices/server code, so foundation beats tooling. Not a driver: it wraps no external engine (vendored, repo-owned). No name collision (no @beep/jsonc today). IMPORTANT: @beep/jsonc depends on @beep/schema, so @beep/schema/Jsonc cannot be re-implemented on top of it (workspace cycle); @beep/schema/Jsonc must be deleted and its consumers moved to @beep/jsonc."}]
- **browserSafe**: True
- **runtimeRequirements**: Pure functions and schemas. Only effectful edge is JsoncFingerprint.hash/hashText requiring core effect/crypto/Digest Digest.Digest in R (no backend shipped; hashResult/hashTextResult take a digest fn). No node:* imports in src (tests use node:crypto createHash as an oracle). Browser-safe and runtime-neutral. Source is ~3.3k top-level lines plus internal/{scanner,parser,navigate,skip,limits}.ts.

- **purpose**: Full minimatch-dialect glob matching (extglobs, braces/sequences, character and POSIX classes, true globstar, negation) compiled to total, pure string predicates. Compilation is the one fallible step and fails with a typed GlobPatternError (PatternTooLong, ExpansionBudgetExceeded, NestingDepthExceeded). The engine is a vendored port of minimatch 10.2.5, brace-expansion 5.0.7 and balanced-match 4.0.4, with every upstream DoS guard kept plus new depth guards. There is no ambient platform detection: platform is an explicit option that defaults to posix.

- **purpose**: Path traversal as Effect primitives. Upward: Walker.ascend, ascendWithin, firstMatch, findUpward, findRoot over a lexical directory chain, with per-probe failure absorption so error channels are never. Downward: descend(GlobPattern, options) expands a compiled glob from its literal prefix into sorted POSIX file paths. It is symlink-safe (an @actions/glob traversalChain cycle guard when followSymlinks is on), supports prune lists and a depth cap, and treats unreadable subtrees as fail, skip or record. compileAndExpand does the same starting from a glob string.
- **proposedPackages**: [{"name": "@beep/walker", "dir": "packages/foundation/capability/walker", "family": "foundation", "kind": "capability", "contents": "Walker.ts, Descend.ts, Expand.ts, internal/worklist.ts, index.ts. Subpath exports: '.', './Walker', './Descend', './Expand'. The README lists named importers to meet the capability gate (@beep/repo-utils Root and FsUtils, @beep/repo-cli, @beep/ai-metrics, plus @beep/box scripts and apps/practice-identify).", "rationale": "I recommend re-deciding the D12 home (tooling/library). Walker is a domain-agnostic technical service over Effect-core FileSystem and Path. It wraps no external engine, SDK or binary, has no product semantics, and is not repo-operational, so it passes the foundation/capability negative gate. It has more than 2 real consumers among the reuse sites below. A tooling/library home breaks real ceilings: drivers can never import tooling (packages/drivers/box/scripts/generate.ts:857 findSdkRoot is a walker site), foundation/capability/file-processing could not use it, and config-file, xdg and workspaces, which depend on walker, could then never move below tooling. Modeling is wrong too: walker performs IO through services, and modeling is for parsers and pure grammars. Its deps fit the capability ceiling (glob is modeling; identity and schema are modeling). Caveat: the modeling package tsconfig-json imports walker (findUpward for nearest-config discovery), and modeling cannot import capability. tsconfig-json must therefore split into pure schemas plus merge semantics (modeling) and discovery/extends-chain IO (a capability or tooling package that may import @beep/walker). Fallback if reviewers reject capability: tooling/library as @beep/repo-walker, accepting that drivers and foundation lose access. No collision with any existing @beep name."}]

- **purpose**: GitHub Actions workflow-command grammar as pure functions. WorkflowCommand renders `::name k=v::message` with the runner's data/property escaping (notice/warning/error/debug/group/endgroup/add-mask/stop-commands helpers, AnnotationProperties schema). CommandNeutralizer makes arbitrary text safe to print so no line can be read as a runner command. Writing to stdout is the caller's job.

It provides one GitHubError taxonomy with `kind` routing, RetryPolicy/RateLimitSnapshot resilience, GitHubApp JWT auth with installation-token mint/rotate/revoke and clientLayer, Repo/RepoRef context, and resource services: GitBranch, GitTag, GitCommit, GitHubCommit, GitHubContent, GitHubIssue, GitHubRelease, GitHubRepository, PullRequest, PullRequestComment, CheckRun, CodeScanning, DeploymentEnvironment, RepositorySecret (libsodium sealed box), RepositoryVariable, RepositorySecurity, Ruleset, TokenPermissions, WorkflowDispatch, Attestation, ArtifactMetadata. Every service ships makeTest/layerTest doubles, and GitHubClient.layerFixture serves recorded responses.

- **purpose**: Managed BEGIN/END sections: marker-delimited blocks that a tool owns inside a file whose surrounding text belongs to the user. SectionDocument is a pure string-to-string core (parse/read/has/check/reconcile/remove, typed SectionParseError on ambiguity, EOL-normalized, byte-preserving outside blocks, BOM-safe). ManagedSection is a thin FileSystem-backed service (read/readAll/isManaged/sync/syncAll/check/checkAll/remove) that writes only when the text changes. CommentStyle presets cover hash/slash/semicolon/dash/html/block. SectionDialect sets the marker phrase and the recognized styles. Markers carry attributes (name="value").

Tests from scratchpad/test/templates (including integration/ and internal/) go to test/ and import through @beep/managed-section.", "rationale": "Re-decide D12: the provisional tooling/library home breaks a family ceiling. github-actions (driver) imports templates in ManagedDocument.ts:2-3 (CommentStyle, SectionDialect, SectionDocument, SectionId, SectionParseError), and drivers may import only foundation primitive/modeling/capability plus other drivers, never tooling. The module is a pure line grammar plus a thin FileSystem adapter, with no engine, SDK or service wrapping and no repo-operational purpose. That makes it foundation, and its imports (identity, schema, utils, effect) sit inside the modeling ceiling. There is precedent for a FileSystem-backed service in modeling: packages/foundation/modeling/ontology/src/TaxonomyLoader.ts and modeling/utils/src/FileSystem.ts. The name 'templates' is misleading (it is not a template engine and invites confusion with codegen-kit). Foundation names are @beep/<purpose>, so the name is @beep/managed-section. No collision with any existing @beep name."}]

No Effect runtime, no IO, no node: imports. ManagedSection.ts is the only effectful file. It needs only effect/FileSystem (core service interface, no platform package) and treats paths as opaque strings, so it needs no Path. Browser-safe apart from the FileSystem layer the consumer provides.

- **purpose**: The GitHub Actions runtime: services an action uses to talk to the runner it runs inside. Action.run composes the runtime, renders failures as ::error:: and sets the exit code. ActionEnvironment snapshots env/GitHubContext/RunnerContext/event payload once. ActionInput gives typed Config inputs. ActionOutputs covers GITHUB_OUTPUT/STEP_SUMMARY/ENV/PATH heredoc writes, setFailed, setSecret and a recording double. ActionState handles cross-phase state and ActionLogger workflow-command logging (groups, annotations, buffered steps, Effect Logger bridge). Secret is the only Redacted unwrap point. ChildEnv, DryRun and DetachedProcess cover child processes. Storage protocols are implemented directly over HTTP with no @actions/*: ActionCache (twirp v2 cache plus azure blob), Artifact, BlobStore (GitHub cache / S3 SigV4), BlobEnvelope, CacheKey (glob-hashed keys). ToolInstaller and PackageManagerInstaller (corepack pins, integrity) install tools. OidcTokenIssuer, the GitHubToken App-token bridge, and the ActionsIdentityToken/ActionsProvenance sbom adapters cover tokens and attestation. A reporting suite (GitHubMarkdown, ManagedDocument, CheckState, CheckDocument) renders PR comments, check runs and the job summary through an injected sink.

Sanctioned node builtins through process.getBuiltinModule: node:child_process and node:fs (DetachedProcess.ts:41-42), node:crypto (internal/digest.ts:9, internal/sigv4.ts:14). ActionEnvironment.ts:521 is the single process.env reader (a snapshot seeded once), enforced by ambientReads.test.ts. @azure/storage-blob is confined to ActionCache.ts, Artifact.ts and BlobStore.githubCache.ts (reachability.test.ts). The reporting suite (GitHubMarkdown, ManagedDocument, CheckState, CheckDocument) is pure apart from Effect/Clock and the injected sink. It imports only markdown, templates, LiteralKit and utils, with no node: or platform imports, so it is browser-safe.

- **purpose**: Typed git introspection and mutation as an Effect service over effect core's ChildProcessSpawner: about 80 Git members (show, lsTree, nameStatus, changedFiles, staged/unstaged/untracked/workingChanges, status, revParse, mergeBase/mergeBaseOption, repoRoot, commonDir, currentBranch, defaultBranch, configGet/GetAll/List, log, commitInfo, worktreeList/Add/Remove, lsFiles, refs, tags, stash, submodule*, fetch/fetchAny/push/pull, commit, add, reset, clean, restore, rm, mv, checkIgnore). A single step classifies failures into typed errors (GitCommandError, NotARepositoryError, UnknownRefError, DirtyWorktreeError, MergeConflictError, NonFastForwardError). Commands run with LC_ALL=C and non-interactive pins (GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0, empty GIT_ASKPASS, ssh BatchMode), parse NUL-terminated output, and stop after a fixed 30 s per run. Also included: GitCommand, which builds about 80 pure, inspectable ChildProcess.Command values and redacts URL userinfo; a pure git-config grammar (GitConfig: offset-preserving parse, diagnostics, edits, includes); and Gitmodules, which parses and decodes .gitmodules on top of GitConfig. The test doubles are Git.makeTest/layerTest (stubs die with NotStubbedError).

- **purpose**: Structured subprocess running and CLI tool discovery over effect core's ChildProcess/ChildProcessSpawner, with no command vocabulary of its own. Run has collect (the one spawn-and-collect primitive: concurrent stdout/stderr/exit drain, byte budget DEFAULT_MAX_OUTPUT_BYTES = 16 MiB), collectTee, text, lines, json, jsonLine, exitCode, succeeds, stream, detach, and extendEnv, which fixes setEnv dropping PATH. Errors are CommandFailedError (kind nonZero, spawn or timeout) and CommandOutputError (notJson, schema or tooLarge), with redacted args and streams. RunOptions carries timeout, redact and maxOutputBytes. ToolDiscovery is a cached service that resolves tools globally or project-locally by spawning them, never via a shell 'command -v', and handles version probing and mismatch policy. Tool and VersionProbe are descriptors. LocalExec is an inverted contract for 'how to run a project-local binary' (launcher prefixes for npm/pnpm/yarn/bun; the implementation lives in the workspaces module). Redaction does value-based secret scrubbing plus a SECRET_FLAGS heuristic. Retry provides transience vocabulary: TRANSIENT_PATTERNS, isTransient, transient(). ScriptedSpawner is a public ChildProcessSpawner test double with notFound/permissionDenied failures.

The only possible confusion is with the directory packages/tooling/tool/cli/src/commands/, which is not a package; @beep/process-runner is a fallback name if reviewers object."}]

- **purpose**: XDG Base Directory resolution: the Xdg service reads HOME, XDG_{CONFIG,DATA,CACHE,STATE}_HOME, XDG_RUNTIME_DIR, XDG_CONFIG_DIRS/XDG_DATA_DIRS, APPDATA/LOCALAPPDATA once at layer construction into an XdgPaths value. AppDirs resolves namespaced config/data/cache/state/runtime directories through a 5-rung precedence (override > XDG var > native (opt-in) > $HOME/<fallbackDir> > $HOME/.<namespace>), with ensure* creation. NativeDirs supplies the macOS/Windows conventions. XdgConfig plugs AppDirs into config-file as resolvers and a savePath.

- **purpose**: Platform-free primitives shared by every front end (CLI / MCP / LSP bin) of a carrier-pattern Effect tool: Distribution (which carrier package installed the bins) with a defaulted Context.Reference, LaunchContext.projectDir (resolve the project dir from argv / ordered env keys / cwd, rejecting empty and unsubstituted ${VAR} values), Remediation (agent-actionable failure hint schema), and ProcessGuard (transport-neutral uncaughtException/unhandledRejection guards installed before a server's module graph loads, with exit policies and a test crash-injection grammar).

- **thirdPartyRuntimeDeps**: ["effect (catalog)", "@beep/identity (workspace; index entry only, guard subpath must stay effect-only)"]
- **browserSafe**: True
- **runtimeRequirements**: Pure: no process reads, no node:* or platform imports, no IO. Distribution/LaunchContext/Remediation are schemas and pure functions (index imports @beep/identity for schema identities). ProcessGuard (guard.ts subpath) imports only effect/* (Effect, Result, Schema, String, Function) by contract so it can load before the server graph; it talks to the process only through the structural ProcessGuardHost the caller passes (Node/Bun `process` satisfies it) and runs Effect.runPromise at the edge. Browser-safe in the sense of no platform imports, though ProcessGuard is only meaningful in a server process.

- **purpose**: Presentation boundary for an effect/cli program. It decides the audience (human, agent or CI) and terminal capability, then handles semantic theme tokens, status vocabulary, glyphs and OSC-8 editor links, a document IR (Doc) with pure plain/ansi/markdown/githubLog renderers, CliMessage status lines, CliLogger (plain lines, everything on stderr) and CliLog (pretty or NDJSON diagnostics, plus an optional file sink), and CliRuntime.main (failure report on stderr, exit codes, Cancelled=130 and NotInteractive=64, CliExit findings codes). It also has prompts that know when nobody can answer (CliPrompt, CliInteractive), SchemaIssueRenderer/ConfigIssueRenderer, Fmt helpers (sanitize, width, truncate, duration, percent, plural) and GithubAnnotation. Optional ./ui adds Ink/React screens and widgets, CliUi.live live views over a Stream/PubSub, and DocView. Test entrypoints are ./testing (CliTest spawns a built bin hermetically; TestTerminal is a Terminal double that drives core prompts) and ./ui/testing (CliUiTest render/view/session/live plus a Vitest snapshot serializer). It is not a parser: effect/cli still owns flags, the command tree and help.

Many makeNoop uses are deliberate deny-all stubs (ProcessTable.ts JSDoc examples, runtime-root/yeet-review-fixes) and should stay."}, {"existing": "ts-morph Project({ useInMemoryFileSystem: true }) in docgen and cli tests", "relation": "complementary", "notes": "ts-morph's own virtual FS for the compiler host, not Effect FileSystem; not replaceable."}]
